<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For a simple, separable, unital, infinite-dimensional, nuclear, stably finite <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra with traces, real rank zero of the uniform tracial ultrapower of its uniform tracial completion implies uniform property Γ. This answers Problem XXI of Schafhauser, Tikuisis and White affirmatively. Independently, strict comparison implies Jiang–Su absorption for simple, separable, unital, infinite-dimensional nuclear algebras, resolving the unital Toms–Winter conjecture. Under comparison tested on traces of finite target rank, we also obtain uniform property Γ and absorption for simple, separable, nuclear, stably projectionless algebras whose densely finite traces are all bounded and have a nonempty compact normalized base.

- **claim-completion-central-halving** (theorem, [source](#stmt-r-thm-main)): Let $A$ be a unital, separable, simple, infinite-dimensional, nuclear, stably finite $C^*$-algebra with $T(A)\ne\varnothing$, and let $B=\overline A^{T(A)}$. Fix any free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$. If $B^\omega$ has real rank zero in its quotient $C^*$-norm, then there is a projection $p\in B^\omega\cap B'$ such that $$\lambda(px)=\tfrac12\lambda(x) \qquad(x\in B,\ \lambda\in\Lambda_\omega).$$ In particular, $A$ has uniform property $\Gamma$.
  - Explanation: Real rank zero of the completion ultrapower supplies a projection central over the completion that halves every designated coefficient trace, establishing uniform property Gamma.
- **claim-completion-trace-identification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-r-cor-completion-traces)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#r:thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="r:thm:main">1</a>, including real rank zero of $B^\omega$ in its quotient $C^*$-norm, every tracial state of $B$ is the designated extension of a unique trace in $T(A)$. Thus restriction is an affine homeomorphism $T(B)\to T(A)$, with inverse $\tau\mapsto\bar\tau$. Moreover, $$\|\sigma(b)\|\le\\|b\\|_{2,T(A)} \qquad(b\in B,\ \sigma\in T(B)),$$ so every tracial state of $B$ is continuous for the designated uniform $2$-norm.
  - Explanation: All abstract tracial states of the completion are designated extensions. Restriction identifies the trace spaces and every such trace satisfies the designated uniform tracial continuity bound.
- **claim-finite-central-halving** (construction, [source](#stmt-r-thm-finite)): Let $A\subseteq D$ be a unital inclusion of $C^*$-algebras, with $A$ nuclear, $D$ of real rank zero, and $T(D)\ne\varnothing$. Suppose that for every $\delta>0$ there is a full projection $g\in D$ with $\sup_{\tau\in T(D)}\tau(g)<\delta$. For every finite $K\subseteq\mathcal U(A)$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there is a projection $P\in D$ such that, for every $x\in K$, $$\sup_{\tau\in T(D)}\\|[P,x]\\|_{2,\tau}<\varepsilon, \qquad \sup_{\tau\in T(D)}\|\tau(Px)-\tfrac12\tau(x)\|<\varepsilon.$$
  - Explanation: A nuclear subalgebra inside a real rank zero ambient algebra with uniformly small full projections admits approximate central coefficient halving on any prescribed finite unitary set.
- **claim-unital-comparison-absorption** (theorem, [source](#stmt-u-thm-main)): Let $A$ be a simple, separable, unital, infinite-dimensional nuclear $C^*$-algebra with strict comparison in the preceding sense. Then $A\cong A\otimes\mathcal Z$. When $T(A)\ne\varnothing$, the proof constructs uniform property $\Gamma$ directly.
  - Explanation: Strict comparison in the stated convention implies Jiang–Su absorption in the unital regime. The tracial case is obtained by constructing uniform property Gamma.
- **claim-projectionless-comparison-absorption** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-thm-main)): Let $A$ be a nonzero simple, separable, nuclear $C^*$-algebra such that $A\otimes\mathcal K$ has no nonzero projections. Suppose that every trace in $T_{\mathrm{lsc}}(A)$ is bounded on the positive unit ball and that $T_1(A)$ is nonempty and weak-$*$ compact. Assume that, for $a,b\in(A\otimes\mathcal K)_+$ with $b\ne0$, $$d_\tau(a)<1\quad \text{for every }\tau\in T_{\mathrm{lsc}}(A) \text{ with }d_\tau(b)=1 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad a\precsim b.$$ Then $A$ has uniform property $\Gamma$ and $A\cong A\otimes\mathcal Z$.
  - Explanation: The stably projectionless route obtains uniform property Gamma and Jiang–Su absorption under boundedness of the densely finite trace cone, compactness of its normalized base, and comparison tested at finite target rank.
- **claim-real-rank-corner-compressions** (construction, [source](#stmt-r-proj-compress)): Every projection corner $rDr$ has real rank zero. Moreover, given a projection $r\in D$, a finite set $\mathcal F\subset D$, and $\gamma>0$, there is a finite family of nonzero orthogonal projections $q_1,\ldots,q_m$ with sum $r$ such that $$\label{r:proj:compress-estimate} \operatorname{dist}(q_j hq_j,\mathbb Cq_j)<\gamma \qquad(h\in\mathcal F,\ 1\le j\le m).$$ The family is empty when $r=0$.
  - Explanation: Real rank zero passes to projection corners and permits finite refinement into approximately scalar compression blocks. The zero corner has an empty family.
- **claim-small-full-positive-support** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-lem-thin-full)): Let $A$ be simple, unital and infinite dimensional, with $T(A)\ne\varnothing$. For every $\delta>0$ there is a full positive element $e\in A$ with $\norm e=1$ such that $$\tau(\operatorname{supp}(e))<\delta\qquad(\tau\in T(A)).$$ Here supports and traces are taken in each tracial von Neumann representation.
  - Explanation: Simplicity and infinite dimensionality provide a full positive element whose support has uniformly small trace. This preliminary result requires neither nuclearity nor comparison.
- **claim-small-full-projections** (construction, [source](#stmt-r-proj-small)): Suppose that $D$ contains a unital simple infinite-dimensional $C^*$-subalgebra $A$. For every $\delta>0$, there is a full projection $g\in D$ such that $$\sup_{\tau\in\operatorname T(D)}\tau(g)<\delta.$$
  - Explanation: A simple infinite-dimensional unital subalgebra supplies small positive supports; real rank zero in the ambient algebra turns them into full projections with uniformly small traces.
- **claim-column-projection-partition** (construction, [source](#stmt-r-proj-columns)): Let $f,g_1,\ldots,g_m$ be projections in $D$, where $m\ge1$. Suppose that $v_1,\ldots,v_m\in D$ satisfy $$v_i=g_i v_i f, \qquad \sum_{i=1}^m v_i^*v_i=f.$$ There are orthogonal projections $e_1,\ldots,e_m$ with sum $f$ and $e_i\precsim g_i$ for every $i$.
  - Explanation: A column identity can be split into orthogonal projections, each subequivalent inside the ambient algebra to its designated target.
- **claim-uniform-proportional-subprojection** (construction, [source](#stmt-r-proj-split)): Assume that for every $\delta>0$ there is a full projection $g\in D$ with $\sup_{\tau\in\operatorname T(D)}\tau(g)<\delta$. For every projection $f\in D$, every $s\in[0,1]$, and every $\varepsilon>0$, there is a projection $e\le f$ such that $$\label{r:proj:split-estimate} \sup_{\tau\in\operatorname T(D)}\|\tau(e)-s\tau(f)\|<\varepsilon.$$
  - Explanation: An ordered partition and a common rounding rule split any projection in a prescribed proportion uniformly over all ambient traces.
- **claim-finite-coefficient-subprojection** (construction, [source](#stmt-r-proj-tests)): Under the small-full-projection hypothesis of Proposition <a href="#r:proj:split" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="r:proj:split">10</a>, let $q\in D$ be a projection, $s\in[0,1]$, and $\mathcal F\subset D$ a finite set. For every $\varepsilon>0$, there is a projection $e\le q$ such that $$\label{r:proj:tests-estimate} \|\tau(eh)-s\tau(qh)\|<\varepsilon \qquad(h\in\mathcal F,\ \tau\in\operatorname T(D)).$$
  - Explanation: Scalar compression followed by proportional splitting preserves finitely many linear trace tests, uniformly over all ambient traces.
- **claim-controlled-orthogonal-insertion** (construction, [source](#stmt-r-proj-move)): For projections $z,y\in D$, there is a projection $t\le1-z$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \tau(y)-3\tau(yz)&\le\tau(t)\le\tau(y), \label{r:proj:move-mass}\\ \left\\|t-y\right\\|_{2,\tau}^{2}&\le5\tau(yz) \label{r:proj:move-distance} \end{aligned}$$ for every $\tau\in\operatorname T(D)$. For every contraction $x\in D$, it also satisfies $$\label{r:proj:move-linear} \|\tau((t-y)x)\| \le2\sqrt{\tau(y)}\,\left\\|t-y\right\\|_{2,\tau}.$$
  - Explanation: A projection can be moved into the unoccupied corner while its loss of trace, tracial displacement, and coefficient-trace error are controlled by its overlap with the occupied projection.
- **claim-nuclear-trace-controlled-averaging** (construction, [source](#stmt-r-weighted-diagonal)): For a finite $K\subseteq\mathcal U(A)$ and $\mu>0$, there are contractions $v_1,\ldots,v_m\in A$ and numbers $\lambda_l\geq0$ with $\sum_l\lambda_l=1$ such that the map $$\Phi(b)=\sum_{l=1}^m\lambda_l v_lbv_l^*,\qquad b\in D,$$ satisfies, for every projection $q\in D$, every $x\in K$, and every $\tau\in\operatorname T(D)$, $$\begin{aligned} 0\leq\Phi(q)&\leq1,\qquad \tau(q)-\mu\leq\tau(\Phi(q))\leq\tau(q),\label{r:eq:diagonal-mass}\\ \left\\|\Phi(q)-x\Phi(q)x^*\right\\|_{1,\tau} &\leq\mu\tau(q).\label{r:eq:diagonal-central} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Nuclear averaging chooses its coefficients before the input projection and controls both the tracial mass loss and approximate centrality of every projected input.
- **claim-tracial-square-root-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-r-weighted-square-root)): For positive $b,c\in D$ and $\tau\in\operatorname T(D)$, $$\left\\|\sqrt b-\sqrt c\right\\|_{2,\tau}^2 \leq\left\\|b-c\right\\|_{1,\tau}.$$
  - Explanation: The squared tracial distance between positive square roots is bounded by the trace-norm distance of the original positive elements.
- **claim-weighted-matrix-blocks** (construction, [source](#stmt-r-weighted-family)): Let $K\subseteq\mathcal U(A)$ be finite and $0<\eta<1$. There are contractions $v_1,\ldots,v_m\in A$ and nonnegative numbers $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_m$ summing to one, chosen using only $K,\eta$, such that the map $$\label{r:eq:averaging-map} \Phi:D\longrightarrow D,\qquad \Phi(b)=\sum_{l=1}^m\lambda_l v_lbv_l^*,$$ satisfies <a href="#r:eq:diagonal-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="r:eq:diagonal-mass">[r:eq:diagonal-mass]</a> with $\mu=\eta^2$. For every projection $r\in D$, there is a finite family consisting of weights $0<w_j\leq1$, nonzero projections $q_j\leq r$, integers $k_j\geq1$, and rows $U_j\in M_{1,k_j}(D)$, such that, with $p_j=U_jU_j^*$, $$\begin{aligned} U_j^*U_j&=1_{k_j}\otimes q_j,\label{r:eq:block-isometry}\\ \operatorname{dist}\big(U_j^*xU_j,M_{k_j}(\mathbb C)\otimes q_j\big)&<\eta &&(x\in K),\label{r:eq:block-compression}\\ \left\\|\sum_jw_jp_j-\Phi(r)\right\\|_{1,\tau}&\leq\eta^2, \label{r:eq:block-mass}\\ \sum_jw_j\left\\|[x,p_j]\right\\|_{2,\tau}^2&\leq6\eta &&(x\in K).\label{r:eq:block-energy} \end{aligned}$$ The last two estimates hold for every $\tau\in\operatorname T(D)$. The family may be empty; the base projections $q_j$ may repeat, and the projections $p_j$ need not be mutually orthogonal.
  - Explanation: An averaging map fixed independently of its input admits weighted projection blocks with nearly scalar matrix compressions and controlled total weighted mass and commutator energy. The blocks need not be mutually orthogonal.
- **claim-matrix-block-sampling** (bound, [source](#stmt-r-packing-sampling)): Let $q\in D$ be a nonzero projection and let $U\in M_{1,k}(D)$ satisfy $U^*U=1_k\otimes q$. Put $p=UU^*$. Suppose $x\in\mathcal U(D)$, $0<\eta<1$, and $$\operatorname{dist}(U^*xU,M_k(\mathbb C)\otimes q)<\eta.$$ For a projection $e\leq q$, set $y=U(1_k\otimes e)U^*$. Then, for $0\leq c\leq1$, every $\tau\in\operatorname T(D)$, and $\delta_\tau=\|\tau(y)-c\tau(p)\|$, $$\begin{aligned} \|\tau(yx)-c\tau(px)\| &\leq\eta\big(\tau(y)+c\tau(p)\big)+2\delta_\tau, \label{r:eq:sampling-linear}\\ \left\|\left\\|[x,y]\right\\|_{2,\tau}^2 -c\left\\|[x,p]\right\\|_{2,\tau}^2\right\| &\leq6\eta\big(\tau(y)+c\tau(p)\big)+10\delta_\tau. \label{r:eq:sampling-energy} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Sampling a base subprojection simultaneously in matrix coordinates preserves coefficient traces and squared commutator energy up to compression and mass-discrepancy errors. Centrality of the sampled subprojection is unnecessary.
- **claim-orthogonal-sum-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-r-packing-orthogonal)): If $t_1,\ldots,t_M$ are mutually orthogonal projections and $z=\sum_a t_a$, then for $x\in D$ and $\tau\in\operatorname T(D)$, $$\left\\|[x,z]\right\\|_{2,\tau}^2 \leq\sum_a\left\\|[x,t_a]\right\\|_{2,\tau}^2.$$ The same bound holds for a sum over any subfamily.
  - Explanation: The commutator energy of an orthogonal projection sum is bounded by the sum of the individual energies, including sums over subfamilies.
- **claim-limit-trace-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-r-completion-detection)): For a bounded sequence $d=(d_j)$ in $B$, with class $[d]\in B^\omega$, one has $$\begin{aligned} \sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_\omega}\lambda([d]^*[d]) &=\lim_{j\to\omega}\sup_{\tau\in\operatorname T(A)} \bar\tau(d_j^*d_j),\label{r:eq:limit-two}\\ \sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_\omega}\|\lambda([d])\| &=\lim_{j\to\omega}\sup_{\tau\in\operatorname T(A)} \|\bar\tau(d_j)\|.\label{r:eq:limit-linear} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, if $\left\\|z\right\\|_{2,\lambda}=0$ for every $\lambda\in\Lambda_\omega$, then $z=0$ in $B^\omega$.
  - Explanation: Varying limit traces recover the coordinate suprema of quadratic and linear trace tests. Vanishing of every designated tracial norm therefore detects the zero ultrapower element.
- **claim-comparison-convention-compatibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-u-lem-comparison-convention)): Let $A$ be simple and unital with $T(A)\ne\varnothing$. Strict comparison in Definition <a href="#u:def:strict-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="u:def:strict-comparison">19</a> implies the functional formulation just stated.
  - Explanation: Strict comparison by tracial dimension functions entails the functional comparison convention needed by the absorption criterion, including targets with infinite rank at some traces.
- **claim-bounded-tracial-diagonal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-u-lem-tracial-diagonal)): Fix finitely many variables $x_1,\ldots,x_m$. For each variable prescribe one of the following domains, with a fixed radius $0\le R_i<\infty$: the closed ball of $D$, its selfadjoint part, or its positive part. Let $(P_k)_{k\ge1}$ be countably many $*$-polynomials in these variables with parameters in $D$. For each $k$ choose a test $$t_k(x)=\norm{P_k(x)}_{2,\Lambda} \quad\text{or}\quad t_k(x)=\sup_{\tau\in\Lambda}\|\tau(P_k(x))\|,$$ and a number $c_k\ge0$. Suppose that for every $n\ge1$ and every $\varepsilon>0$ there is an admissible tuple $x\in D^m$ with $$t_k(x)<c_k+\varepsilon\qquad(1\le k\le n).$$ Then there is an admissible tuple with $t_k(x)\le c_k$ for every $k$. In particular, when every $c_k=0$, all the prescribed tracial equations hold exactly. A polynomial with zero uniform $2$-norm is zero as an element of $D$.
  - Explanation: Approximate satisfiability of every finite set of tracial polynomial tests yields a simultaneous solution in fixed norm balls. A polynomial with vanishing uniform tracial norm is zero in the quotient.
- **claim-gamma-functional-comparison-absorption** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-u-thm-gamma-criterion)): Let $A$ be simple, separable, unital and nuclear, with $T(A)\ne\varnothing$. If $A$ has strict comparison in the functional formulation and uniform property $\Gamma$, then $$A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z.$$
  - Explanation: The cited absorption criterion combines functional strict comparison with uniform property Gamma in the simple separable unital nuclear tracial regime.
- **claim-unital-trace-rank-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-prop-rank-approximation)): Let $A$ be a simple, unital, infinite-dimensional $C^*$-algebra with $T=T(A)\ne\varnothing$. For every $b\in A_+$ and $\delta>0$ there are an integer $L\ge1$ and $v\in M_L(A)_+$ such that $$\tau(b)-\delta<d_\tau(v)\le\tau(b) \qquad(\tau\in T). \label{u:eq:rank-approximation}$$ This statement requires neither separability, nuclearity nor strict comparison. There is no asserted bound on $L$ independent of $b$ and $\delta$.
  - Explanation: Trace-preserving compression into small hereditary support and integer spectral cuts approximate trace functions by finite-matrix support ranks. No uniform matrix-size bound is asserted.
- **claim-hereditary-almost-projections** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-lem-almost-projection)): For $b\in A_+$ with $\norm{b}<1$ and $\delta>0$, there is a positive contraction $p_0\in\operatorname{her}(b)$ such that, for every $\tau\in T$, $$\tau(b)-2\delta<\tau(p_0)\le\tau(b), \qquad 0\le\tau(p_0-p_0^2)<2\delta. \label{u:eq:almost-projection}$$
  - Explanation: Strict comparison places a rank approximant in a prescribed hereditary subalgebra. Functional calculus then produces a positive contraction with the desired approximate trace and a small projection defect.
- **claim-ultrapower-spectral-projection-supply** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-prop-projection-supply)): For every positive contraction $h\in D$ and $0<\eta<1$, there is a projection $p\in D$ such that $$p\le\mathbf 1_{[\eta,1]}(h)\quad\text{in }D^{**}, \qquad \tau(p)=\tau((h-\eta)_+) \quad(\tau\in\Lambda). \label{u:eq:projection-supply}$$ In addition, for $k_\eta(s)=\min\{s/\eta,1\}$ on $[0,1]$, one has $$k_\eta(h)p=p=pk_\eta(h)\quad\text{in }D. \label{u:eq:projection-cutoff}$$
  - Explanation: Hereditary almost projections become exact projections in the tracial ultrapower, with prescribed cut trace and an exact continuous support identity. The spectral order comparison is in the bidual.
- **claim-corner-trace-zero-symmetry** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-cor-corner-symmetry)): For every projection $q\in D$ there is $r=r^*\in qDq$ such that $$r^2=q,\qquad \tau(r)=0\quad(\tau\in\Lambda).$$
  - Explanation: Halving a projection in trace gives a selfadjoint symmetry in its corner with zero trace for every designated limit trace.
- **claim-large-trace-scalar-compressions** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-lem-scalar-compressions)): Let $q\in D$ be a projection, let $x_1,\ldots,x_n\in D$ be selfadjoint contractions, and let $\varepsilon,\delta>0$. There are finitely many pairwise orthogonal projections $p_1,\ldots,p_L\le q$ and numbers $\mu_{s,j}\in[-1,1]$ such that $$\label{u:eq:scalar-compressions} \begin{split} \norm{p_sx_jp_s-\mu_{s,j}p_s}&\le\varepsilon \quad(1\le j\le n,\ 1\le s\le L),\\ \tau\!\left(q-\sum_{s=1}^L p_s\right)&\le\delta \quad(\tau\in\Lambda). \end{split}$$ The family may be empty. No commutation of the $p_s$ with the $x_j$ is asserted.
  - Explanation: Finite scalar compression blocks cover all but an arbitrarily small trace of the initial projection. These blocks are not asserted to commute with the coefficients.
- **claim-finite-coefficient-centered-symmetry** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-lem-coefficient-symmetry)): For a projection $q\in D$, a finite set $H\subset D$, and $\zeta>0$, there is $r=r^*\in qDq$ such that $$\label{u:eq:coefficient-symmetry} r^2=q, \qquad \sup_{\tau\in\Lambda}\|\tau(br)\|<\zeta \quad(b\in H).$$
  - Explanation: Trace-zero corner symmetries on scalar compression blocks give a symmetry whose trace pairing with a prescribed finite coefficient family is uniformly small.
- **claim-finite-dimensional-commutant-centering** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-prop-centered-symmetry)): Let $E\subset D$ be a finite-dimensional $C^*$-subalgebra, possibly nonunital, and let $S\subset D$ be a unital norm-separable $C^*$-subalgebra. There is a selfadjoint unitary $r\in D\cap E'$ such that $$\label{u:eq:centered-symmetry} \tau(sr)=0\qquad(s\in S,\ \tau\in\Lambda).$$
  - Explanation: Matrix-unit transport and bounded diagonalization give an exact selfadjoint unitary commuting with the finite-dimensional algebra and centered against the separable coefficient algebra. Commutation with the coefficient algebra is not asserted.
- **claim-nuclear-convex-order-zero-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-u-thm-hkw-approximation)): Let $N$ be a nuclear $C^*$-algebra, let $K\subset N$ be finite, and let $\varepsilon>0$. There are a finite-dimensional $C^*$-algebra $F$ and completely positive contractive maps $$N\xrightarrow{\psi}F\xrightarrow{\phi}N$$ such that $\norm{\phi\psi(x)-x}<\varepsilon$ for $x\in K$, and $$\phi=\sum_{i=1}^{M}\beta_i\phi_i, \qquad \beta_i\ge0,\qquad \sum_{i=1}^{M}\beta_i=1,$$ where each $\phi_i:F\to N$ is completely positive, contractive, and of order zero. The number $M$ is finite for the chosen approximation; no bound on $M$ independent of $K$ and $\varepsilon$ is asserted.
  - Explanation: The cited nuclear approximation theorem factors finite-set approximations through a finite-dimensional algebra with a convex combination of outgoing order-zero maps. The number of summands has no asserted uniform bound.
- **claim-order-zero-homomorphism-replacement** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-lem-homomorphism-replacement)): Let $F$ be finite dimensional, let $\phi:F\to D$ be a completely positive contractive order-zero map, and put $h=\phi(1_F)$. For $0<\eta<1$ there is a possibly nonunital $*$-homomorphism $\rho:F\to D$ such that, writing $P=\rho(1_F)$, for every $\tau\in\Lambda$, $$\tau(P)\ge\tau(h)-\eta, \qquad \norm{\phi(c)-\rho(c)}_{2,\tau}^{2} \le 4(1-\tau(h))+2\eta \quad (c\in F,\ \norm{c}\le1). \label{u:eq:homomorphism-replacement}$$
  - Explanation: Spectral projection supply transports supporting matrix units into the ultrapower, replacing an order-zero map by a possibly nonunital homomorphism with trace-weighted error control.
- **claim-averaged-centered-observable** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-prop-averaged-observable)): Let $E\subset A$ be a finite set of selfadjoint contractions and let $0<\eta<1$. There is a selfadjoint $z\in D$ such that, for every $\tau\in\Lambda$, $$\begin{aligned} \norm{[z,a]}_{2,\tau}^{2}&\le72\eta &&(a\in E),\\ \tau(bz)&=0,\qquad \tau(bz^2)=\tau(b) &&(b\in A),\\ \tau(z^4)&\le3. \end{aligned} \label{u:eq:averaged-observable}$$ The construction gives $z=\sum_{i=1}^M\sqrt{\beta_i}\,r_i$, where the $r_i$ are selfadjoint unitaries, $\beta_i\ge0$, and $\sum_i\beta_i=1$. In fact, $\tau(z^4)\le3-2\sum_i\beta_i^2$. There is no asserted norm bound on $z$ independent of $M$.
  - Explanation: A square-root-weighted sum of successively centered symmetries has exact coefficient moments, small commutator energy, and a controlled fourth moment. Its norm need not be bounded independently of the approximation.
- **claim-tracial-clipping-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-u-lem-clipping)): Let $z=z^*\in D$, let $R>0$, and set $$g_R(s)=\max(-R,\min(s,R)),\qquad d=g_R(z).$$ For every $a\in D$ and $\tau\in\Lambda$, $$\norm{[d,a]}_{2,\tau}\le\norm{[z,a]}_{2,\tau}. \label{u:eq:clipping-commutator}$$ Moreover, for every contraction $b\in D$, $$\begin{aligned} \|\tau(b(d-z))\|&\le R^{-3}\tau(z^4),\\ \|\tau(b(d^2-z^2))\|&\le R^{-2}\tau(z^4),\\ \tau(d^4)&\le\tau(z^4). \end{aligned} \label{u:eq:clipping-tails}$$ In particular, if $z$ is obtained from Proposition <a href="#u:prop:averaged-observable" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="u:prop:averaged-observable">33</a>, then $\norm{d}\le R$ and, for every $\tau\in\Lambda$, $$\begin{aligned} \norm{[d,a]}_{2,\tau}&\le\sqrt{72\eta} &&(a\in E),\\ \|\tau(bd)\|&\le3/R^3 &&(b\in A,\ \norm{b}\le1),\\ \|\tau(b(d^2-1))\|&\le3/R^2 &&(b\in A,\ \norm{b}\le1),\\ \tau(d^4)&\le3 \end{aligned} \label{u:eq:clipped-moments}$$
  - Explanation: Clipping a selfadjoint observable does not increase tracial commutator norms. Its coefficient moment losses are controlled by the fourth moment and the clipping radius.
- **claim-local-bounded-moments** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-prop-local-moments)): Fix $R\ge1$. For every finite set $E\subset A$ of selfadjoint contractions, every finite set $H\subset A$ of contractions, and every $\sigma>0$, there is a selfadjoint $d\in A$ with $\norm{d}\le R$ such that, simultaneously, $$\begin{aligned} \norm{[d,a]}_{2,T}&<\sigma &&(a\in E),\\ \sup_{\tau\in T}\|\tau(bd)\|&\le3/R^3+\sigma &&(b\in H),\\ \sup_{\tau\in T}\|\tau(b(d^2-1))\|&\le3/R^2+\sigma &&(b\in H),\\ \sup_{\tau\in T}\tau(d^4)&\le3+\sigma. \end{aligned} \label{u:eq:local-moments}$$
  - Explanation: A good coordinate of a bounded lift realizes the clipped moment estimates directly in the original algebra, with a freely small additional finite-set error.
- **claim-relative-bounded-moments** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-prop-relative-moments)): Let $C\subset D$ be a unital norm-separable $C^*$-subalgebra and fix $R\geq1$. There is a selfadjoint element $d\in D\cap C'$ with $\norm{d}\leq R$ such that, for every $b\in C$ with $\norm{b}\leq1$ and every $\tau\in\Lambda$, $$\label{u:eq:relative-moments} \|\tau(bd)\|\leq\frac{3}{R^3},\qquad \|\tau(b(d^2-1))\|\leq\frac{3}{R^2},\qquad \tau(d^4)\leq3.$$
  - Explanation: Coordinatewise local constructions transfer bounded moment estimates to the relative commutant of any norm-separable coefficient algebra. Nuclearity is not required of that coefficient algebra.
- **claim-conditional-gaussian-rows** (construction, [source](#stmt-u-prop-conditional-clt)): For every integer $N\geq1$ there exist mutually commuting selfadjoint elements $d_{N,1},\ldots,d_{N,N}\in D\cap A'$, each of norm at most $N$, with the following properties. Put $$C_{N,j}=C^*(A,d_{N,1},\ldots,d_{N,j-1}), \qquad s_N=\frac{1}{\sqrt N}\sum_{j=1}^N d_{N,j}.$$ For every contraction $b\in C_{N,j}$ and $\tau\in\Lambda$, $$\label{u:eq:row-moments} \|\tau(bd_{N,j})\|\leq\frac{3}{N^3},\qquad \|\tau(b(d_{N,j}^2-1))\|\leq\frac{3}{N^2},\qquad \tau(d_{N,j}^4)\leq3.$$ For every fixed $t\in\mathbb R$, $$\label{u:eq:uniform-characteristic-functions} \lim_{N\to\infty} \sup_{\substack{\tau\in\Lambda\\a\in A,\ \norm{a}\leq1}} \left\|\tau(ae^{its_N})-e^{-t^2/2}\tau(a)\right\|=0.$$
  - Explanation: Successively enlarging the coefficient algebra constructs commuting rows with conditional moment control. Their normalized sums converge in coefficient characteristic functions to the Gaussian law, uniformly over traces and the coefficient unit ball.
- **claim-uniform-coefficient-weak-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-u-lem-uniform-weak-convergence)): For every fixed positive contraction $a\in A$ and every fixed bounded continuous function $f:\mathbb R\to\mathbb C$, $$\label{u:eq:uniform-weak-convergence} \lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{\tau\in\Lambda} \left\|\tau(af(s_N))-\tau(a)\int_{\mathbb R}f\,d\gamma\right\|=0.$$
  - Explanation: The characteristic-function limit gives weak convergence against each fixed bounded continuous function and fixed positive coefficient, uniformly over the varying designated traces.
- **claim-unital-uniform-gamma-partitions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-u-thm-uniform-gamma)): Let $A$ be a simple, separable, unital, infinite-dimensional nuclear $C^*$-algebra with nonempty tracial-state space and strict comparison as in Definition <a href="#u:def:strict-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="u:def:strict-comparison">19</a>. For every integer $m\geq1$ there exist pairwise orthogonal projections $p_1,\ldots,p_m\in D\cap A'$ such that $$\label{u:eq:gamma-projections} \sum_{i=1}^m p_i=1, \qquad \tau(ap_i)=\frac{\tau(a)}{m} \quad(a\in A,\ \tau\in\Lambda,\ 1\leq i\leq m).$$ In particular, $A$ has uniform property $\Gamma$.
  - Explanation: Continuous approximations to equal-measure Gaussian intervals give bounded approximate partitions. A tracial diagonal argument yields exact central projection partitions preserving every coefficient trace.
- **claim-nonunital-tracial-quotient-unit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-lem-unit)): The algebra $D$ is unital, $\Lambda$ consists of tracial states, and every $\tau\in \Lambda$ restricts to an element of $T$. Moreover, $$\\|[(a_n)]\\|_{2,\Lambda}=\lim_\omega\\|a_n\\|_{2,T},$$ and this norm detects zero in $D$.
  - Explanation: Compactness of the normalized trace base makes an approximate unit uniform in trace, producing a unit in the tracial quotient. The designated limit traces are states and their uniform norm detects zero.
- **claim-nonunital-trace-rank-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-n-lem-rank)): For $g\in A_+$ and $\xi>0$ there is a positive contraction $k$ in a finite matrix algebra over $A$ such that, for every $\sigma\in T$, $$\label{n:eq:rank} \|\sigma(k)-\sigma(g)\|<\xi,\qquad 0\le d_\sigma(k)-\sigma(k)<\xi.$$
  - Explanation: Trace transfer into small hereditary support and clipped spectral layers approximate a positive element's trace by a matrix positive contraction with small excess support rank.
- **claim-nonunital-supported-projection-selection** (construction, [source](#stmt-n-prop-selection)): Let $h,z,a\in D$ be positive contractions satisfying $hz=z$ and $\tau(a)\le\tau(z)$ for all $\tau\in \Lambda$. There is a projection $p\in D$ such that $$hp=p,\qquad \tau(p)=\tau(a)\quad(\tau\in \Lambda).$$
  - Explanation: Finite-target-rank comparison with an auxiliary matrix buffer selects a projection of prescribed limit trace, supported exactly where the given positive contraction acts as the unit.
- **claim-corner-proportional-cost-selection** (construction, [source](#stmt-n-lem-cost)): Let $q\in D$ be a projection, $Y\in(qDq)_+$, $0<t<1$, and $\varepsilon>0$. There is a projection $r\le q$ such that, for $\tau\in \Lambda$, $$\label{n:eq:cost-corner} \tau(r)=t\tau(q),\qquad \tau(rY)\le t\bigl(\tau(Y)+\varepsilon\tau(q)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Spectral allocation chooses a prescribed fraction of a projection while bounding its positive observable cost by the same fraction of the total cost, with a chosen tolerance.
- **claim-finite-dimensional-commutant-cost** (construction, [source](#stmt-n-cor-cost)): If $E\subset D$ is finite dimensional and unital, $Y\in D_+$, $0<t<1$, and $\varepsilon>0$, there is a projection $p\in D\cap E'$ with $$\label{n:eq:cost} \tau(pd)=t\tau(d)\quad(d\in E),\qquad \tau(pY)\le t\bigl(\tau(Y)+\varepsilon\bigr)\quad(\tau\in \Lambda).$$
  - Explanation: Corner cost selection repeated along matrix units yields a projection commuting with a finite-dimensional algebra, splitting all its coefficient traces and controlling a positive cost.
- **claim-small-local-central-splittings** (construction, [source](#stmt-n-prop-small)): Let $0<\lambda<1$, let $\mathcal F$ be a finite set of self-adjoint contractions in $A$, and let $\rho>0$. There is a positive contraction $e\in A$ such that, for $\sigma\in T$ and $x\in\mathcal F$, $$\label{n:eq:small} \begin{split} \\|[e,x]\\|_{2,\sigma}&<\rho,\\ \|\sigma(ex)-\lambda\sigma(x)\|&<\lambda^{3/2}+\rho,\\ \sigma(e-e^2)&<\lambda^{3/2}+\rho. \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: Weighted models, a combined overlap-and-error cost, and truncation produce small approximate central splittings directly in the algebra. The trace and projection errors have the stated superlinear scale.
- **claim-weighted-finite-dimensional-models** (construction, [source](#stmt-n-prop-models)): For a finite set $\mathcal F$ of self-adjoint contractions in $A$ and $\Delta>0$, there are unital finite-dimensional subalgebras $D_i\subset D$, self-adjoint contractions $x^{(i)}\in D_i$ for each $x\in\mathcal F$, and positive weights $w_i$ summing to one, such that $$\label{n:eq:models} E_i=\sum_{x\in\mathcal F}(x-x^{(i)})^2, \qquad \sum_iw_i\tau(E_i)<\Delta\quad(\tau\in \Lambda).$$
  - Explanation: Hyperfinite models at individual traces are convexly averaged and replaced by finite-dimensional subalgebras of the quotient. Their total weighted squared error is uniformly small.
- **claim-projection-sum-truncation** (bound, [source](#stmt-n-lem-truncation)): Let $p_1,\ldots,p_N$ be projections in a unital $C^*$-algebra with a tracial state $\tau$. Put $$s=\sum_ip_i,\qquad e=\min(s,1),\qquad \lambda=\sum_i\tau(p_i),\qquad R=\sum_i\tau\Bigl(p_i\sum_{j<i}p_j\Bigr).$$ For every self-adjoint $x$, $$\label{n:eq:trunc-comm} \\|[e,x]\\|_{2,\tau} \le2\Bigl(2\sum_i\\|[p_i,x]\\|_{2,\tau}^2\Bigr)^{1/2}.$$ Moreover, $$\label{n:eq:trunc-trace} \tau(s-e),\ \tau(e-e^2)\le\sqrt{2\lambda R}.$$
  - Explanation: Truncating a sum of projections controls its commutator by aggregate squared commutators and bounds both discarded trace and projection defect using total trace mass and overlap. The bounds have no model-count factor.
- **claim-relative-small-central-splittings** (construction, [source](#stmt-n-lem-relative-small)): For every separable unital $E\subset D$ and $0<\lambda<1$, there is a positive contraction $e\in D\cap E'$ such that, for $\tau\in \Lambda$, $$\label{n:eq:relative-small} \|\tau(ex)-\lambda\tau(x)\|\le\lambda^{3/2} \quad(x\in E_{\mathrm{sa}},\ \\|x\\|\le1), \qquad \tau(e-e^2)\le\lambda^{3/2}.$$
  - Explanation: Coordinatewise local splittings yield a positive contraction commuting with any separable unital coefficient algebra and satisfying uniform small-proportion trace and projection estimates.
- **claim-exact-relative-proportional-splitting** (construction, [source](#stmt-n-prop-split)): For every separable unital $E\subset D$ and $0<c<1$, there is a projection $f\in D\cap E'$ such that $$\label{n:eq:split} \tau(fx)=c\tau(x)\qquad(x\in E,\ \tau\in \Lambda).$$
  - Explanation: Multiplication of commuting complements amplifies small splittings to any fixed proportion. Bounded diagonalization removes the remaining trace and projection errors.
- **claim-projectionless-uniform-gamma-partitions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-thm-gamma)): For every $n\ge2$ there are orthogonal projections $f_1,\ldots,f_n\in D\cap A'$ summing to $1_D$ such that $$\tau(f_i a)=\frac1n\tau(a)\qquad(a\in A,\ \tau\in \Lambda).$$ In particular, $A$ has uniform property $\Gamma$.
  - Explanation: Successive exact splittings in enlarged coefficient algebras produce orthogonal central projection partitions with equal coefficient traces in the nonunital tracial quotient.
- **claim-central-lifts-and-annihilator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-lem-central-lift)): The quotient $F_\omega(A)$ is unital, the annihilator is contained in $J$, and the map $A_\omega\cap A'\to D\cap A'$ is onto. Consequently, for each $n$ there is an order-zero map $\psi:M_n\to A_\omega\cap A'$ satisfying $\psi(1)+J=1_D$. Denote its composition with the quotient map to $F_\omega(A)$ by $\varphi$.
  - Explanation: The annihilator quotient is unital, its annihilator maps to zero in the tracial quotient, and central elements of the tracial quotient have norm-central lifts. Matrix maps therefore lift as order-zero maps with full tracial image.
- **claim-generalized-trace-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-lem-generalized)): Every generalized limit trace $\nu$ finite at a fixed nonzero $a\in A_+$ is a finite nonnegative multiple of a limit trace through $D$. The induced maps $\varphi$ in Lemma <a href="#n:lem:central-lift" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="n:lem:central-lift">51</a> are therefore tracially large for all the required Pedersen-ideal tests.
  - Explanation: Boundedness of densely finite traces and compactness of the normalized base uniformly bound the coordinate scalar normalizations. Generalized limit traces finite on a fixed nonzero element thus factor through the tracial quotient, proving the required largeness of the lifted matrix maps.

        "exact_quote": "For projections $z,y\\in D$, there is a projection $t\\le1-z$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n  \\tau(y)-3\\tau(yz)&\\le\\tau(t)\\le\\tau(y),\n       \\label{r:proj:move-mass}\\\\\n  \\left\\|t-y\\right\\|_{2,\\tau}^{2}&\\le5\\tau(yz)\n       \\label{r:proj:move-distance}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for every $\\tau\\in\\operatorname T(D)$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-r-weighted-diagonal",
        "exact_quote": "For a finite $K\\subseteq\\mathcal U(A)$ and $\\mu>0$, there are contractions $v_1,\\ldots,v_m\\in A$ and numbers $\\lambda_l\\geq0$ with $\\sum_l\\lambda_l=1$ such that the map $$\\Phi(b)=\\sum_{l=1}^m\\lambda_l v_lbv_l^*,\\qquad b\\in D,$$ satisfies, for every projection $q\\in D$, every $x\\in K$, and every $\\tau\\in\\operatorname T(D)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0\\leq\\Phi(q)&\\leq1,\\qquad\n \\tau(q)-\\mu\\leq\\tau(\\Phi(q))\\leq\\tau(q),\\label{r:eq:diagonal-mass}\\\\\n \\left\\|\\Phi(q)-x\\Phi(q)x^*\\right\\|_{1,\\tau}\n &\\leq\\mu\\tau(q).\\label{r:eq:diagonal-central}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-r-weighted-diagonal",
          "exact_quote": "For a finite $K\\subseteq\\mathcal U(A)$ and $\\mu>0$, there are contractions $v_1,\\ldots,v_m\\in A$ and numbers $\\lambda_l\\geq0$ with $\\sum_l\\lambda_l=1$ such that the map $$\\Phi(b)=\\sum_{l=1}^m\\lambda_l v_lbv_l^*,\\qquad b\\in D,$$ satisfies, for every projection $q\\in D$, every $x\\in K$, and every $\\tau\\in\\operatorname T(D)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0\\leq\\Phi(q)&\\leq1,\\qquad\n \\tau(q)-\\mu\\leq\\tau(\\Phi(q))\\leq\\tau(q),\\label{r:eq:diagonal-mass}\\\\\n \\left\\|\\Phi(q)-x\\Phi(q)x^*\\right\\|_{1,\\tau}\n &\\leq\\mu\\tau(q).\\label{r:eq:diagonal-central}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-291.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For a finite $K\\subseteq\\mathcal U(A)$ and $\\mu>0$, there are contractions $v_1,\\ldots,v_m\\in A$ and numbers $\\lambda_l\\geq0$ with $\\sum_l\\lambda_l=1$ such that the map $$\\Phi(b)=\\sum_{l=1}^m\\lambda_l v_lbv_l^*,\\qquad b\\in D,$$ satisfies, for every projection $q\\in D$, every $x\\in K$, and every $\\tau\\in\\operatorname T(D)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0\\leq\\Phi(q)&\\leq1,\\qquad\n \\tau(q)-\\mu\\leq\\tau(\\Phi(q))\\leq\\tau(q),\\label{r:eq:diagonal-mass}\\\\\n \\left\\|\\Phi(q)-x\\Phi(q)x^*\\right\\|_{1,\\tau}\n &\\leq\\mu\\tau(q).\\label{r:eq:diagonal-central}\n\\end{aligned}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-r-weighted-family",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K\\subseteq\\mathcal U(A)$ be finite and $0<\\eta<1$. There are contractions $v_1,\\ldots,v_m\\in A$ and nonnegative numbers $\\lambda_1,\\ldots,\\lambda_m$ summing to one, chosen using only $K,\\eta$, such that the map $$\\label{r:eq:averaging-map}\n \\Phi:D\\longrightarrow D,\\qquad\n \\Phi(b)=\\sum_{l=1}^m\\lambda_l v_lbv_l^*,$$ satisfies <a href=\"#r:eq:diagonal-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"r:eq:diagonal-mass\">[r:eq:diagonal-mass]</a> with $\\mu=\\eta^2$. For every projection $r\\in D$, there is a finite family consisting of weights $0<w_j\\leq1$, nonzero projections $q_j\\leq r$, integers $k_j\\geq1$, and rows $U_j\\in M_{1,k_j}(D)$, such that, with $p_j=U_jU_j^*$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n U_j^*U_j&=1_{k_j}\\otimes q_j,\\label{r:eq:block-isometry}\\\\\n \\operatorname{dist}\\big(U_j^*xU_j,M_{k_j}(\\mathbb C)\\otimes q_j\\big)&<\\eta\n       &&(x\\in K),\\label{r:eq:block-compression}\\\\\n \\left\\|\\sum_jw_jp_j-\\Phi(r)\\right\\|_{1,\\tau}&\\leq\\eta^2,\n       \\label{r:eq:block-mass}\\\\\n \\sum_jw_j\\left\\|[x,p_j]\\right\\|_{2,\\tau}^2&\\leq6\\eta\n       &&(x\\in K).\\label{r:eq:block-energy}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The last two estimates hold for every $\\tau\\in\\operatorname T(D)$. The family may be empty; the base projections $q_j$ may repeat, and the projections $p_j$ need not be mutually orthogonal.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-r-weighted-family",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K\\subseteq\\mathcal U(A)$ be finite and $0<\\eta<1$. There are contractions $v_1,\\ldots,v_m\\in A$ and nonnegative numbers $\\lambda_1,\\ldots,\\lambda_m$ summing to one, chosen using only $K,\\eta$, such that the map $$\\label{r:eq:averaging-map}\n \\Phi:D\\longrightarrow D,\\qquad\n \\Phi(b)=\\sum_{l=1}^m\\lambda_l v_lbv_l^*,$$ satisfies <a href=\"#r:eq:diagonal-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"r:eq:diagonal-mass\">[r:eq:diagonal-mass]</a> with $\\mu=\\eta^2$. For every projection $r\\in D$, there is a finite family consisting of weights $0<w_j\\leq1$, nonzero projections $q_j\\leq r$, integers $k_j\\geq1$, and rows $U_j\\in M_{1,k_j}(D)$, such that, with $p_j=U_jU_j^*$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n U_j^*U_j&=1_{k_j}\\otimes q_j,\\label{r:eq:block-isometry}\\\\\n \\operatorname{dist}\\big(U_j^*xU_j,M_{k_j}(\\mathbb C)\\otimes q_j\\big)&<\\eta\n       &&(x\\in K),\\label{r:eq:block-compression}\\\\\n \\left\\|\\sum_jw_jp_j-\\Phi(r)\\right\\|_{1,\\tau}&\\leq\\eta^2,\n       \\label{r:eq:block-mass}\\\\\n \\sum_jw_j\\left\\|[x,p_j]\\right\\|_{2,\\tau}^2&\\leq6\\eta\n       &&(x\\in K).\\label{r:eq:block-energy}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The last two estimates hold for every $\\tau\\in\\operatorname T(D)$. The family may be empty; the base projections $q_j$ may repeat, and the projections $p_j$ need not be mutually orthogonal.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $K\\subseteq\\mathcal U(A)$ be finite and $0<\\eta<1$. There are contractions $v_1,\\ldots,v_m\\in A$ and nonnegative numbers $\\lambda_1,\\ldots,\\lambda_m$ summing to one, chosen using only $K,\\eta$, such that the map $$\\label{r:eq:averaging-map}\n \\Phi:D\\longrightarrow D,\\qquad\n \\Phi(b)=\\sum_{l=1}^m\\lambda_l v_lbv_l^*,$$ satisfies <a href=\"#r:eq:diagonal-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"r:eq:diagonal-mass\">[r:eq:diagonal-mass]</a> with $\\mu=\\eta^2$. For every projection $r\\in D$, there is a finite family consisting of weights $0<w_j\\leq1$, nonzero projections $q_j\\leq r$, integers $k_j\\geq1$, and rows $U_j\\in M_{1,k_j}(D)$, such that, with $p_j=U_jU_j^*$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n U_j^*U_j&=1_{k_j}\\otimes q_j,\\label{r:eq:block-isometry}\\\\\n \\operatorname{dist}\\big(U_j^*xU_j,M_{k_j}(\\mathbb C)\\otimes q_j\\big)&<\\eta\n       &&(x\\in K),\\label{r:eq:block-compression}\\\\\n \\left\\|\\sum_jw_jp_j-\\Phi(r)\\right\\|_{1,\\tau}&\\leq\\eta^2,\n       \\label{r:eq:block-mass}\\\\\n \\sum_jw_j\\left\\|[x,p_j]\\right\\|_{2,\\tau}^2&\\leq6\\eta\n       &&(x\\in K).\\label{r:eq:block-energy}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The last two estimates hold for every $\\tau\\in\\operatorname T(D)$. The family may be empty; the base projections $q_j$ may repeat, and the projections $p_j$ need not be mutually orthogonal.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $q\\in D$ be a nonzero projection and let $U\\in M_{1,k}(D)$ satisfy $U^*U=1_k\\otimes q$. Put $p=UU^*$. Suppose $x\\in\\mathcal U(D)$, $0<\\eta<1$, and $$\\operatorname{dist}(U^*xU,M_k(\\mathbb C)\\otimes q)<\\eta.$$ For a projection $e\\leq q$, set $y=U(1_k\\otimes e)U^*$. Then, for $0\\leq c\\leq1$, every $\\tau\\in\\operatorname T(D)$, and $\\delta_\\tau=|\\tau(y)-c\\tau(p)|$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n |\\tau(yx)-c\\tau(px)|\n &\\leq\\eta\\big(\\tau(y)+c\\tau(p)\\big)+2\\delta_\\tau,\n \\label{r:eq:sampling-linear}\\\\\n \\left|\\left\\|[x,y]\\right\\|_{2,\\tau}^2\n        -c\\left\\|[x,p]\\right\\|_{2,\\tau}^2\\right|\n &\\leq6\\eta\\big(\\tau(y)+c\\tau(p)\\big)+10\\delta_\\tau.\n \\label{r:eq:sampling-energy}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-r-packing-orthogonal",
        "exact_quote": "If $t_1,\\ldots,t_M$ are mutually orthogonal projections and $z=\\sum_a t_a$, then for $x\\in D$ and $\\tau\\in\\operatorname T(D)$, $$\\left\\|[x,z]\\right\\|_{2,\\tau}^2\n \\leq\\sum_a\\left\\|[x,t_a]\\right\\|_{2,\\tau}^2.$$ The same bound holds for a sum over any subfamily.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-r-completion-detection",
        "exact_quote": "For a bounded sequence $d=(d_j)$ in $B$, with class $[d]\\in B^\\omega$, one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\Lambda_\\omega}\\lambda([d]^*[d])\n &=\\lim_{j\\to\\omega}\\sup_{\\tau\\in\\operatorname T(A)}\n             \\bar\\tau(d_j^*d_j),\\label{r:eq:limit-two}\\\\\n \\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\Lambda_\\omega}|\\lambda([d])|\n &=\\lim_{j\\to\\omega}\\sup_{\\tau\\in\\operatorname T(A)}\n             |\\bar\\tau(d_j)|.\\label{r:eq:limit-linear}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, if $\\left\\|z\\right\\|_{2,\\lambda}=0$ for every $\\lambda\\in\\Lambda_\\omega$, then $z=0$ in $B^\\omega$.",

      },

          "source_id": "PM-291.4"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-u-lem-tracial-diagonal",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many variables $x_1,\\ldots,x_m$. For each variable prescribe one of the following domains, with a fixed radius $0\\le R_i<\\infty$: the closed ball of $D$, its selfadjoint part, or its positive part. Let $(P_k)_{k\\ge1}$ be countably many $*$-polynomials in these variables with parameters in $D$. For each $k$ choose a test $$t_k(x)=\\norm{P_k(x)}_{2,\\Lambda}\n \\quad\\text{or}\\quad\n t_k(x)=\\sup_{\\tau\\in\\Lambda}|\\tau(P_k(x))|,$$ and a number $c_k\\ge0$. Suppose that for every $n\\ge1$ and every $\\varepsilon>0$ there is an admissible tuple $x\\in D^m$ with $$t_k(x)<c_k+\\varepsilon\\qquad(1\\le k\\le n).$$ Then there is an admissible tuple with $t_k(x)\\le c_k$ for every $k$. In particular, when every $c_k=0$, all the prescribed tracial equations hold exactly. A polynomial with zero uniform $2$-norm is zero as an element of $D$.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.4"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a simple, unital, infinite-dimensional $C^*$-algebra with $T=T(A)\\ne\\varnothing$. For every $b\\in A_+$ and $\\delta>0$ there are an integer $L\\ge1$ and $v\\in M_L(A)_+$ such that $$\\tau(b)-\\delta<d_\\tau(v)\\le\\tau(b)\n \\qquad(\\tau\\in T).\n \\label{u:eq:rank-approximation}$$ This statement requires neither separability, nuclearity nor strict comparison.       },
      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be finite dimensional, let $\\phi:F\\to D$ be a completely positive contractive order-zero map, and put $h=\\phi(1_F)$. For $0<\\eta<1$ there is a possibly nonunital $*$-homomorphism $\\rho:F\\to D$ such that, writing $P=\\rho(1_F)$, for every $\\tau\\in\\Lambda$, $$\\tau(P)\\ge\\tau(h)-\\eta,\n \\qquad\n \\norm{\\phi(c)-\\rho(c)}_{2,\\tau}^{2}\n \\le 4(1-\\tau(h))+2\\eta\n \\quad (c\\in F,\\ \\norm{c}\\le1).\n \\label{u:eq:homomorphism-replacement}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-u-lem-homomorphism-replacement",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be finite dimensional, let $\\phi:F\\to D$ be a completely positive contractive order-zero map, and put $h=\\phi(1_F)$.           "source_id": "PM-291.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $F$ be finite dimensional, let $\\phi:F\\to D$ be a completely positive contractive order-zero map, and put $h=\\phi(1_F)$.         "anchor": "stmt-u-prop-averaged-observable",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E\\subset A$ be a finite set of selfadjoint contractions and let $0<\\eta<1$. There is a selfadjoint $z\\in D$ such that, for every $\\tau\\in\\Lambda$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n  \\norm{[z,a]}_{2,\\tau}^{2}&\\le72\\eta &&(a\\in E),\\\\\n  \\tau(bz)&=0,\\qquad \\tau(bz^2)=\\tau(b) &&(b\\in A),\\\\\n  \\tau(z^4)&\\le3.\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{u:eq:averaged-observable}$$ The construction gives $z=\\sum_{i=1}^M\\sqrt{\\beta_i}\\,r_i$, where the $r_i$ are selfadjoint unitaries, $\\beta_i\\ge0$, and $\\sum_i\\beta_i=1$.         "source_id": "PM-291.4"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-u-lem-clipping",
        "exact_quote": "Let $z=z^*\\in D$, let $R>0$, and set $$g_R(s)=\\max(-R,\\min(s,R)),\\qquad d=g_R(z).$$ For every $a\\in D$ and $\\tau\\in\\Lambda$, $$\\norm{[d,a]}_{2,\\tau}\\le\\norm{[z,a]}_{2,\\tau}.\n \\label{u:eq:clipping-commutator}$$ Moreover, for every contraction $b\\in D$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n  |\\tau(b(d-z))|&\\le R^{-3}\\tau(z^4),\\\\\n  |\\tau(b(d^2-z^2))|&\\le R^{-2}\\tau(z^4),\\\\\n  \\tau(d^4)&\\le\\tau(z^4).\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{u:eq:clipping-tails}$$ In particular, if $z$ is obtained from Proposition <a href=\"#u:prop:averaged-observable\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"u:prop:averaged-observable\">33</a>, then $\\norm{d}\\le R$ and, for every $\\tau\\in\\Lambda$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n  \\norm{[d,a]}_{2,\\tau}&\\le\\sqrt{72\\eta} &&(a\\in E),\\\\\n  |\\tau(bd)|&\\le3/R^3 &&(b\\in A,\\ \\norm{b}\\le1),\\\\\n  |\\tau(b(d^2-1))|&\\le3/R^2 &&(b\\in A,\\ \\norm{b}\\le1),\\\\\n  \\tau(d^4)&\\le3\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{u:eq:clipped-moments}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-u-lem-clipping",
          "exact_quote": "Let $z=z^*\\in D$, let $R>0$, and set $$g_R(s)=\\max(-R,\\min(s,R)),\\qquad d=g_R(z).$$ For every $a\\in D$ and $\\tau\\in\\Lambda$, $$\\norm{[d,a]}_{2,\\tau}\\le\\norm{[z,a]}_{2,\\tau}.\n \\label{u:eq:clipping-commutator}$$ Moreover, for every contraction $b\\in D$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n  |\\tau(b(d-z))|&\\le R^{-3}\\tau(z^4),\\\\\n  |\\tau(b(d^2-z^2))|&\\le R^{-2}\\tau(z^4),\\\\\n  \\tau(d^4)&\\le\\tau(z^4).\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{u:eq:clipping-tails}$$ In particular, if $z$ is obtained from Proposition <a href=\"#u:prop:averaged-observable\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"u:prop:averaged-observable\">33</a>, then $\\norm{d}\\le R$ and, for every $\\tau\\in\\Lambda$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n  \\norm{[d,a]}_{2,\\tau}&\\le\\sqrt{72\\eta} &&(a\\in E),\\\\\n  |\\tau(bd)|&\\le3/R^3 &&(b\\in A,\\ \\norm{b}\\le1),\\\\\n  |\\tau(b(d^2-1))|&\\le3/R^2 &&(b\\in A,\\ \\norm{b}\\le1),\\\\\n  \\tau(d^4)&\\le3\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{u:eq:clipped-moments}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-291.4"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-u-prop-local-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $R\\ge1$. For every finite set $E\\subset A$ of selfadjoint contractions, every finite set $H\\subset A$ of contractions, and every $\\sigma>0$, there is a selfadjoint $d\\in A$ with $\\norm{d}\\le R$ such that, simultaneously, $$\\begin{aligned}\n  \\norm{[d,a]}_{2,T}&<\\sigma &&(a\\in E),\\\\\n  \\sup_{\\tau\\in T}|\\tau(bd)|&\\le3/R^3+\\sigma &&(b\\in H),\\\\\n  \\sup_{\\tau\\in T}|\\tau(b(d^2-1))|&\\le3/R^2+\\sigma &&(b\\in H),\\\\\n  \\sup_{\\tau\\in T}\\tau(d^4)&\\le3+\\sigma.\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{u:eq:local-moments}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.4"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-291.4"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-u-prop-relative-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C\\subset D$ be a unital norm-separable $C^*$-subalgebra and fix $R\\geq1$. There is a selfadjoint element $d\\in D\\cap C'$ with $\\norm{d}\\leq R$ such that, for every $b\\in C$ with $\\norm{b}\\leq1$ and every $\\tau\\in\\Lambda$, $$\\label{u:eq:relative-moments}\n  |\\tau(bd)|\\leq\\frac{3}{R^3},\\qquad\n  |\\tau(b(d^2-1))|\\leq\\frac{3}{R^2},\\qquad\n  \\tau(d^4)\\leq3.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.4"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-u-prop-conditional-clt",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $N\\geq1$ there exist mutually commuting selfadjoint elements $d_{N,1},\\ldots,d_{N,N}\\in D\\cap A'$, each of norm at most $N$, with the following properties. Put $$C_{N,j}=C^*(A,d_{N,1},\\ldots,d_{N,j-1}),\n  \\qquad\n  s_N=\\frac{1}{\\sqrt N}\\sum_{j=1}^N d_{N,j}.$$ For every contraction $b\\in C_{N,j}$ and $\\tau\\in\\Lambda$, $$\\label{u:eq:row-moments}\n  |\\tau(bd_{N,j})|\\leq\\frac{3}{N^3},\\qquad\n  |\\tau(b(d_{N,j}^2-1))|\\leq\\frac{3}{N^2},\\qquad\n  \\tau(d_{N,j}^4)\\leq3.$$ For every fixed $t\\in\\mathbb R$, $$\\label{u:eq:uniform-characteristic-functions}\n  \\lim_{N\\to\\infty}\n  \\sup_{\\substack{\\tau\\in\\Lambda\\\\a\\in A,\\ \\norm{a}\\leq1}}\n  \\left|\\tau(ae^{its_N})-e^{-t^2/2}\\tau(a)\\right|=0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-u-prop-conditional-clt",
          "exact_quote": "For every integer $N\\geq1$ there exist mutually commuting selfadjoint elements $d_{N,1},\\ldots,d_{N,N}\\in D\\cap A'$, each of norm at most $N$, with the following properties.           "source_id": "PM-291.4"

      "exact_statement": "For every integer $N\\geq1$ there exist mutually commuting selfadjoint elements $d_{N,1},\\ldots,d_{N,N}\\in D\\cap A'$, each of norm at most $N$, with the following properties. Put $$C_{N,j}=C^*(A,d_{N,1},\\ldots,d_{N,j-1}),\n  \\qquad\n  s_N=\\frac{1}{\\sqrt N}\\sum_{j=1}^N d_{N,j}.$$ For every contraction $b\\in C_{N,j}$ and $\\tau\\in\\Lambda$, $$\\label{u:eq:row-moments}\n  |\\tau(bd_{N,j})|\\leq\\frac{3}{N^3},\\qquad\n  |\\tau(b(d_{N,j}^2-1))|\\leq\\frac{3}{N^2},\\qquad\n  \\tau(d_{N,j}^4)\\leq3.$$ For every fixed $t\\in\\mathbb R$, $$\\label{u:eq:uniform-characteristic-functions}\n  \\lim_{N\\to\\infty}\n  \\sup_{\\substack{\\tau\\in\\Lambda\\\\a\\in A,\\ \\norm{a}\\leq1}}\n  \\left|\\tau(ae^{its_N})-e^{-t^2/2}\\tau(a)\\right|=0.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-u-lem-uniform-weak-convergence",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-u-thm-uniform-gamma",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a simple, separable, unital, infinite-dimensional nuclear $C^*$-algebra with nonempty tracial-state space and strict comparison as in Definition <a href=\"#u:def:strict-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"u:def:strict-comparison\">19</a>.         "exact_quote": "For a finite set $\\mathcal F$ of self-adjoint contractions in $A$ and $\\Delta>0$, there are unital finite-dimensional subalgebras $D_i\\subset D$, self-adjoint contractions $x^{(i)}\\in D_i$ for each $x\\in\\mathcal F$, and positive weights $w_i$ summing to one, such that $$\\label{n:eq:models}\n E_i=\\sum_{x\\in\\mathcal F}(x-x^{(i)})^2,\n \\qquad \\sum_iw_i\\tau(E_i)<\\Delta\\quad(\\tau\\in \\Lambda).$$",

        "exact_quote": "Let $p_1,\\ldots,p_N$ be projections in a unital $C^*$-algebra with a tracial state $\\tau$.       "exact_statement": "Let $p_1,\\ldots,p_N$ be projections in a unital $C^*$-algebra with a tracial state $\\tau$. <!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Full projection** ([source](#r:sec:preliminaries)): A projection is *full* if the closed two-sided ideal it generates is the whole algebra.
- **Projection subequivalence inside the ambient algebra** ([source](#r:sec:preliminaries)): For projections $e,f\in D$, the notation $e\precsim f$ means that there is a partial isometry $v\in D$ with $v^*v=e$ and $vv^*\leq f$.
- **Real rank zero** ([source](#r:sec:preliminaries)): Real rank zero means that finite-spectrum self-adjoint elements are norm dense in $D_{\mathrm{sa}}$.
- **Uniform tracial completion** ([source](#pm-h-6)): The uniform tracial completion is $B=C/J$, equipped with its quotient $C^*$-norm.
- **Designated traces on the completion** ([source](#pm-h-6)): The designated traces on $B$ are precisely $$\bar\tau([(a_j)])=\lim_j\tau(a_j),\qquad \tau\in\operatorname T(A).$$
- **Completion uniform tracial ultrapower** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For a fixed free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$, define $$\label{r:eq:ultrapower} B^\omega=\ell^\infty(\mathbb N,B)/I_\omega,\qquad I_\omega=\{(b_j):\lim_{j\to\omega}\left\\|b_j\right\\|_{2,\operatorname T(A)}=0\}.$$
- **Varying limit trace on the completion ultrapower** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For each sequence $(\tau_j)$ in $\operatorname T(A)$, its *limit trace* is $$\label{r:eq:limit-trace} \lambda([(b_j)])=\lim_{j\to\omega}\bar\tau_j(b_j).$$
- **Completion formulation of uniform property Gamma** ([source](#r:eq:gamma-definition)): We say that $B$ has property $\Gamma$, or equivalently that $A$ has *uniform property $\Gamma$*, if there is a projection $p\in B^\omega\cap B'$ such that $$\label{r:eq:gamma-definition} \lambda(px)=\tfrac12\lambda(x) \quad(x\in B,\ \lambda\in\Lambda_\omega).$$
- **Cuntz subequivalence in the stabilization** ([source](#pm-h-21)): For $a,b\in(A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal K)_+$, Cuntz subequivalence $a\precsim b$ means that there are $x_n\in A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal K$ with $$\norm{x_n^*bx_n-a}\longrightarrow0.$$
- **Cuntz semigroup** ([source](#pm-h-21)): Its classes form the ordered abelian semigroup $\mathop{\mathrm{Cu}}(A)$, with $[a]\le[b]$ when $a\precsim b$ and addition given by orthogonal direct sum.
- **Tracial dimension function** ([source](#pm-h-21)): Set $$d_\tau(a)=\lim_{n\to\infty}(\tau\otimes\operatorname{Tr})(a^{1/n}) \in[0,\infty].$$
- **Strict comparison** ([source](#stmt-u-def-strict-comparison)): The algebra $A$ has strict comparison if, whenever $a,b\in(A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal K)_+$ and $b\ne0$, the inequalities $$d_\tau(a)<d_\tau(b)\qquad(\tau\in T(A))$$ imply $a\precsim b$.
- **Uniform property Gamma by coefficient partitions** ([source](#stmt-u-def-uniform-gamma)): For a simple separable unital $A$ with $T(A)\ne\varnothing$, uniform property $\Gamma$ means that for every $m\ge1$ there are projections $p_1,\ldots,p_m\in D\cap A'$ satisfying $$\sum_{i=1}^m p_i=1, \qquad \tau(ap_i)=\frac{1}{m}\tau(a) \quad(a\in A,\ \tau\in\Lambda,\ 1\le i\le m).$$
- **Order-zero completely positive map** ([source](#pm-h-28)): A completely positive map has *order zero* if it preserves orthogonality of positive elements: $xy=0$ for $x,y\ge0$ implies $\phi(x)\phi(y)=0$.
- **Hereditary subalgebra generated by a positive element** ([source](#u:sec:preliminaries)): For a $C^*$-algebra $E$, write $E_+$ for its positive cone, $E_{\mathrm{sa}}$ for its selfadjoint part, and $$\operatorname{her}(b)=\overline{bEb}\qquad(b\in E_+)$$ for the hereditary subalgebra generated by $b$.
- **Norm central sequence annihilator quotient** ([source](#n:sec:absorption)): Let $$A_\omega=\ell^\infty(A)/\{(a_n):\lim_\omega\\|a_n\\|=0\}, \quad J=\ker(A_\omega\to D), \quad F_\omega(A)=(A_\omega\cap A')/(A_\omega\cap A^\perp).$$
- **Generalized limit trace** ([source](#n:eq:generalized)): A generalized limit trace on $A_\omega$ comes from lower-semicontinuous traces $\nu_n$ on $A$ by $$\label{n:eq:generalized} \nu([(b_n)])=\sup_{\epsilon>0}\lim_\omega\nu_n((b_n-\epsilon)_+) \quad(b_n\ge0).$$
- **Almost unperforated Cuntz semigroup** ([source](#n:sec:absorption)): It is *almost unperforated* if $(k+1)[x]\le k[y]$ implies $[x]\le[y]$ for every integer $k\ge1$.

<!-- PM:END pm.objects.v2 -->

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-completion-halving | Coefficient multiplying the designated trace in the completion splitting identity | = | $\tfrac12$ | The same central projection works for every completion coefficient and every designated limit trace. |
| q-orthogonal-insertion-distance | Squared tracial displacement of the inserted projection | ≤ | $5\tau(yz)$ | The overlap is measured at the same trace as the displacement. |
| q-weighted-block-energy | Total weighted squared commutator norm of the matrix-block projections | ≤ | $6\eta$ | The averaging coefficients are chosen before the input projection; the bound is uniform over the finite unitary tests. |
| q-packing-energy-increment | Increment of the cumulative packing energy budget | = | $1000\eta(E_{i-1}+\eta)$ | The recurrence starts with vanishing initial energy and is iterated through the prescribed rounds. |
| q-packing-halving-error | Uniform coefficient-trace halving error of the packed projection | ≤ | $\tfrac12e^{-H}+5H\eta+\tfrac32E_N+\sqrt{15HE_N}$ | First choose the mass-exhaustion parameter; then increase the number of rounds with that parameter fixed. |
| q-unital-rank-approximation-error | Trace value minus the approximating finite-matrix support rank | < | $\delta$ | The approximating rank never exceeds the trace; its matrix size may depend on the element and tolerance. |
| q-hereditary-projection-defect | Trace of the positive contraction's projection defect | < | $2\delta$ | The defect estimate and trace approximation hold uniformly over the tracial state space. |
| q-averaged-observable-commutator | Squared tracial commutator norm of the averaged observable | ≤ | $72\eta$ | The error is controlled on the prescribed finite selfadjoint coefficient family. |
| q-averaged-observable-fourth-moment | Fourth tracial moment of the averaged observable | ≤ | $3$ | The moment bound is independent of the number of summands; the norm bound is not. |
| q-clipped-first-coefficient-moment | Absolute first coefficient moment of the clipped observable | ≤ | $3/R^3$ | The radius is fixed before passage to local or relative bounded moments. |
| q-clipped-second-coefficient-moment | Absolute second coefficient moment defect of the clipped observable | ≤ | $3/R^2$ | This retained error is controlled by the fixed radius and is not removed by reducing the additional local tolerance. |
| q-conditional-row-first-moment | Absolute conditional first coefficient moment of a row variable | ≤ | $\frac{3}{N^3}$ | The coefficient algebra includes all earlier variables in the row. |
| q-conditional-row-second-moment | Absolute conditional second coefficient moment defect of a row variable | ≤ | $\frac{3}{N^2}$ | The estimate holds for all designated limit traces and contractions in the earlier coefficient algebra. |
| q-nonunital-local-projection-defect | Trace of the local small splitting's projection defect | < | $\lambda^{3/2}+\rho$ | The local tolerance can decrease at fixed splitting proportion; the superlinear proportion error remains for amplification. |
| q-truncated-sum-defects | Discarded trace and projection defect of a truncated projection sum | ≤ | $\sqrt{2\lambda R}$ | The mass is the sum of the projection traces and the overlap is measured against earlier projections in the same family. |
| q-relative-small-projection-defect | Trace of the relative small splitting's projection defect | ≤ | $\lambda^{3/2}$ | The same positive contraction also satisfies the small-proportion coefficient trace estimate. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-corner-compressions** [claim](#stmt-r-proj-compress) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-proj-compress): Spectral approximation in corners followed by finite refinement. -> Approximately scalar projection blocks in any projection corner.
- **step-thin-positive-support** [claim](#stmt-u-lem-thin-full) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-lem-thin-full): Orthogonal positive elements and successive common hereditary support reduction. -> Full positive elements with uniformly small tracial support.
- **step-small-full-projections** [claim](#stmt-r-proj-small) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-proj-small): Finite-spectrum approximation, spectral cut, and proper-quotient contradiction. -> Uniformly small full ambient projections.
- **step-column-splitting** [claim](#stmt-r-proj-columns) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-proj-columns): Induction using spectral cuts and inverse-square-root normalization. -> Orthogonal partition with individual projection subequivalences.
- **step-proportional-subprojections** [claim](#stmt-r-proj-split) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-proj-split): Fullness frame, ordered projection partition, and summation by parts. -> Uniform approximate proportional trace splitting.
- **step-linear-trace-tests** [claim](#stmt-r-proj-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-proj-tests): Scalar compressions and proportional splitting inside each block. -> Subprojection preserving the finite coefficient trace targets.
- **step-orthogonal-insertion** [claim](#stmt-r-proj-move) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-proj-move): Cut the overlap spectrum and use a polar normalization in the remaining corner. -> Orthogonal replacement with trace, displacement, and linear-error estimates.
- **step-nuclear-averaging** [claim](#stmt-r-weighted-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-weighted-diagonal): Virtual diagonal and Hahn–Banach passage from weak to norm closure. -> An averaging map selected independently of the input projection.
- **step-square-root-bound** [claim](#stmt-r-weighted-square-root) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-weighted-square-root): Tracial polar-sign calculation and trace-norm duality. -> Square-root tracial distance bound.
- **step-weighted-blocks** [claim](#stmt-r-weighted-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-weighted-family): Scalar Gram matrices, spectral cutoff, polar normalization, and spectral layer decomposition. -> Weighted matrix blocks with controlled mass and total commutator energy.
- **step-block-sampling** [claim](#stmt-r-packing-sampling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-packing-sampling): Compare matrix compressions with scalar matrices and expand the unitary commutator energy. -> Linear trace and commutator sampling estimates.
- **step-orthogonal-energy** [claim](#stmt-r-packing-orthogonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-packing-orthogonal): Split off-diagonal commutator corners into orthogonal components. -> Energy bound for the sum and every subfamily sum.
- **step-finite-central-packing** [claim](#stmt-r-thm-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-packing-orthogonal-2): Backward preparation of coefficient tests followed by signed sampling, orthogonal insertion, and mass exhaustion. -> Approximate central coefficient-halving projection on a finite unitary set.
- **step-limit-trace-detection** [claim](#stmt-r-completion-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-completion-detection): Choose coordinatewise approximate maximizing traces and take ultralimits. -> Exact detection identities for linear and quadratic trace tests.
- **step-completion-central-projection** [claim](#stmt-r-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-completion-detection-2): Choose bounded good coordinates from finite-stage projections, then extend identities by uniform tracial density. -> A central halving projection over the entire completion for the original ultrafilter.
- **step-completion-all-traces** [claim](#stmt-r-cor-completion-traces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-r-completion-detection-3): Property Gamma, complemented partitions of unity, the type condition, and the cited trace theorem. -> Identification of all completion traces and their uniform tracial continuity.
- **step-functional-comparison** [claim](#stmt-u-lem-comparison-convention) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-lem-comparison-convention): Apply trace-induced semigroup functionals and bound spectral cut ranks in finite matrix corners. -> The functional strict comparison convention required for absorption.
- **step-bounded-tracial-diagonal** [claim](#stmt-u-lem-tracial-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-lem-tracial-diagonal): Fix bounded lifts and select coordinates using decreasing ultrafilter-large sets. -> A simultaneous admissible solution of the tracial inequalities.
- **step-gamma-absorption-interface** [claim](#stmt-u-thm-gamma-criterion) / [proof](#pm-h-24): Invoke the stated external absorption criterion. -> Jiang–Su absorption.
- **step-unital-rank-rounding** [claim](#stmt-u-prop-rank-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-prop-rank-approximation): Extract an exact fullness frame, transfer the trace to small support, and use integer spectral cuts. -> Finite-matrix rank approximation from below.
- **step-hereditary-almost-projections** [claim](#stmt-u-lem-almost-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-lem-almost-projection): Strict comparison, compactness for a uniform rank cut, and continuous functional calculus. -> Hereditary positive contraction with controlled trace and projection defect.
- **step-spectral-projection-supply** [claim](#stmt-u-prop-projection-supply) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-prop-projection-supply): Pass hereditary almost projections to the tracial quotient and retain exact cutoff identities. -> Projection with the prescribed spectral-cut trace and bidual support.
- **step-corner-symmetry** [claim](#stmt-u-cor-corner-symmetry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-cor-corner-symmetry): Halve a corner projection and take the associated selfadjoint symmetry. -> Trace-zero symmetry in every projection corner.
- **step-unital-scalar-compressions** [claim](#stmt-u-lem-scalar-compressions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-lem-scalar-compressions): Repeated spectral partition runs with a moving-corner continuity estimate and finite refinement. -> Approximately scalar orthogonal blocks with small uniform residual trace.
- **step-finite-symmetry-centering** [claim](#stmt-u-lem-coefficient-symmetry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-lem-coefficient-symmetry): Use trace-zero symmetries on scalar compression blocks and absorb the small residual corner. -> A corner symmetry approximately centered against finite coefficients.
- **step-exact-commutant-centering** [claim](#stmt-u-prop-centered-symmetry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-prop-centered-symmetry): Transport corner symmetries along matrix units and diagonalize coefficient tests in a fixed ball. -> Selfadjoint unitary commuting with the finite-dimensional algebra and exactly centered against the coefficient algebra.
- **step-convex-order-zero-approximation** [claim](#stmt-u-thm-hkw-approximation) / [proof](#pm-h-28): Invoke the cited nuclear convex approximation theorem. -> Finite-dimensional completely positive approximation with convex order-zero outgoing maps.
- **step-homomorphism-replacement** [claim](#stmt-u-lem-homomorphism-replacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-lem-homomorphism-replacement): Construct a supporting homomorphism in the bidual and transport cut-supported matrix units into the quotient. -> Possibly nonunital homomorphism with trace-controlled replacement error.
- **step-averaged-observable** [claim](#stmt-u-prop-averaged-observable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-prop-averaged-observable): Weighted approximation estimates, successive symmetry centering, and noncommutative moment expansion. -> Observable with exact coefficient moments and a controlled fourth moment.
- **step-tracial-clipping** [claim](#stmt-u-lem-clipping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-lem-clipping): Joint spectral calculus of left and right multiplication and scalar tail inequalities. -> Bounded observable preserving commutator control with explicit coefficient moment losses.
- **step-local-bounded-moments** [claim](#stmt-u-prop-local-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-prop-local-moments): Clip the averaged observable, choose bounded selfadjoint lifts, and select a good coordinate. -> Local bounded moment estimates in the original algebra.
- **step-relative-bounded-moments** [claim](#stmt-u-prop-relative-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-prop-relative-moments): Apply local estimates to coordinate lifts of dense coefficient families with a common radius. -> A commuting relative observable with bounded conditional moment errors.
- **step-conditional-gaussian-limit** [claim](#stmt-u-prop-conditional-clt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-prop-conditional-clt): Adjoin commuting relative observables successively and telescope the conditional Taylor recurrence. -> Commuting rows whose normalized sums have uniform Gaussian coefficient characteristic functions.
- **step-uniform-weak-convergence** [claim](#stmt-u-lem-uniform-weak-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-lem-uniform-weak-convergence): Contradiction using normalized positive coefficient measures and the Lévy continuity theorem. -> Uniform coefficient weak convergence over the designated traces.
- **step-unital-gamma-partitions** [claim](#stmt-u-thm-uniform-gamma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-thm-uniform-gamma): Approximate equal-measure Gaussian intervals continuously and diagonalize bounded positive partitions. -> Exact central projection partitions with equal coefficient traces.
- **step-unital-absorption** [claim](#stmt-u-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-u-thm-uniform-gamma-2): Apply the Gamma absorption criterion in the tracial case; prove pure infiniteness and use absorption in the empty-trace case. -> Jiang–Su absorption in both trace-space cases.
- **step-nonunital-quotient-unit** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-unit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-unit): Dini uniform convergence of approximate-unit trace values and coordinatewise trace maximization. -> Unital tracial quotient with normalized limit traces and zero detection.
- **step-nonunital-rank-rounding** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-rank): Order-zero matrix lifting supplies thin support; trace transfer and clipped layers control excess support rank. -> Matrix positive contraction approximating trace with small rank excess.
- **step-supported-selection** [claim](#stmt-n-prop-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-prop-selection): Add a small matrix buffer, apply finite-target-rank comparison, then discard the auxiliary corner and pass to the quotient. -> Projection with prescribed trace and exact support identity.
- **step-corner-cost-selection** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-cost): Recursive spectral allocation using supported projection selection. -> Proportional corner projection with a one-sided cost bound.
- **step-commutant-cost-selection** [claim](#stmt-n-cor-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-cor-cost): Average the observable into matrix corners and repeat selected corner projections along matrix diagonals. -> Commuting proportional projection with controlled total cost.
- **step-weighted-models** [claim](#stmt-n-prop-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-prop-models): Pointwise hyperfinite approximation, convex separation on the compact trace base, and supported model replacement. -> Unital finite-dimensional models with small total weighted squared error.
- **step-projection-sum-truncation** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-truncation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-truncation): Block-matrix Lipschitz functional calculus in tracial Hilbert space and support-trace estimates. -> Commutator and defect bounds for the truncated sum.
- **step-small-local-splittings** [claim](#stmt-n-prop-small) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-truncation-2): Select projections using one combined overlap-and-model-error cost, truncate their sum, and choose a good coordinate. -> Small approximate central coefficient splittings in the original algebra.
- **step-relative-small-splittings** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-relative-small) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-relative-small): Coordinatewise application to dense selfadjoint coefficient lifts. -> Commuting relative small splitting with the stated trace and projection errors.
- **step-exact-relative-splittings** [claim](#stmt-n-prop-split) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-prop-split): Multiply successively commuting complements and apply bounded decreasing-set diagonalization. -> Exact projection splitting every coefficient trace in the target proportion.
- **step-nonunital-gamma-partitions** [claim](#stmt-n-thm-gamma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-thm-gamma): Successive relative proportional splitting inside remaining complements. -> Orthogonal central projection partitions with equal coefficient traces.
- **step-central-norm-lifts** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-central-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-central-lift): Approximate-unit quotient unit, annihilator estimate, quasicentral ideal diagonalization, and order-zero lifting. -> Tracially full central order-zero maps in the annihilator quotient.
- **step-generalized-trace-normalization** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-generalized) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-generalized): Normalize densely finite coordinate traces using compactness, then remove spectral regularization with a uniform scalar bound. -> Factorization through the tracial quotient and largeness for Pedersen-ideal tests.
- **step-nonunital-absorption** [claim](#stmt-n-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-generalized-2): Establish uniform McDuffness, derive almost unperforation from finite-target-rank comparison, and apply the nonunital absorption criterion. -> Jiang–Su absorption in the bounded-trace stably projectionless regime.

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-r-packing-orthogonal",
          "exact_quote": "If $t_1,\\ldots,t_M$ are mutually orthogonal projections and $z=\\sum_a t_a$, then for $x\\in D$ and $\\tau\\in\\operatorname T(D)$, $$\\left\\|[x,z]\\right\\|_{2,\\tau}^2\n \\leq\\sum_a\\left\\|[x,t_a]\\right\\|_{2,\\tau}^2.$$ The same bound holds for a sum over any subfamily.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.4"
        }
      ],

      ],
      "method": "Split off-diagonal commutator corners into orthogonal components.",
      "output": "Energy bound for the sum and every subfamily sum.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-r-packing-orthogonal"
    },

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-r-thm-finite",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subseteq D$ be a unital inclusion of $C^*$-algebras, with $A$ nuclear, $D$ of real rank zero, and $T(D)\\ne\\varnothing$. Suppose that for every $\\delta>0$ there is a full projection $g\\in D$ with $\\sup_{\\tau\\in T(D)}\\tau(g)<\\delta$. For every finite $K\\subseteq\\mathcal U(A)$ and $\\varepsilon>0$ there is a projection $P\\in D$ such that, for every $x\\in K$, $$\\sup_{\\tau\\in T(D)}\\|[P,x]\\|_{2,\\tau}<\\varepsilon,\n \\qquad\n \\sup_{\\tau\\in T(D)}|\\tau(Px)-\\tfrac12\\tau(x)|<\\varepsilon.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-291.4"
        }
      ],

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-r-packing-orthogonal-2"
    },

          "source_id": "PM-291.4"
        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-r-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a unital, separable, simple, infinite-dimensional, nuclear, stably finite $C^*$-algebra with $T(A)\\ne\\varnothing$, and let $B=\\overline A^{T(A)}$. Fix any free ultrafilter $\\omega$ on $\\mathbb N$. If $B^\\omega$ has real rank zero in its quotient $C^*$-norm, then there is a projection $p\\in B^\\omega\\cap B'$ such that $$\\lambda(px)=\\tfrac12\\lambda(x)\n \\qquad(x\\in B,\\ \\lambda\\in\\Lambda_\\omega).$$ In particular, $A$ has uniform property $\\Gamma$.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.4"
        }
      ],

    },

          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#r:thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"r:thm:main\">1</a>, including real rank zero of $B^\\omega$ in its quotient $C^*$-norm, every tracial state of $B$ is the designated extension of a unique trace in $T(A)$. Thus restriction is an affine homeomorphism $T(B)\\to T(A)$, with inverse $\\tau\\mapsto\\bar\\tau$. Moreover, $$|\\sigma(b)|\\le\\|b\\|_{2,T(A)}\n \\qquad(b\\in B,\\ \\sigma\\in T(B)),$$ so every tracial state of $B$ is continuous for the designated uniform $2$-norm.",

Let $(P_k)_{k\\ge1}$ be countably many $*$-polynomials in these variables with parameters in $D$. For each $k$ choose a test $$t_k(x)=\\norm{P_k(x)}_{2,\\Lambda}\n \\quad\\text{or}\\quad\n t_k(x)=\\sup_{\\tau\\in\\Lambda}|\\tau(P_k(x))|,$$ and a number $c_k\\ge0$. Suppose that for every $n\\ge1$ and every $\\varepsilon>0$ there is an admissible tuple $x\\in D^m$ with $$t_k(x)<c_k+\\varepsilon\\qquad(1\\le k\\le n).$$ Then there is an admissible tuple with $t_k(x)\\le c_k$ for every $k$. In particular, when every $c_k=0$, all the prescribed tracial equations hold exactly. A polynomial with zero uniform $2$-norm is zero as an element of $D$.",

        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-u-lem-clipping",
          "exact_quote": "Let $z=z^*\\in D$, let $R>0$, and set $$g_R(s)=\\max(-R,\\min(s,R)),\\qquad d=g_R(z).$$ For every $a\\in D$ and $\\tau\\in\\Lambda$, $$\\norm{[d,a]}_{2,\\tau}\\le\\norm{[z,a]}_{2,\\tau}.\n \\label{u:eq:clipping-commutator}$$ Moreover, for every contraction $b\\in D$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n  |\\tau(b(d-z))|&\\le R^{-3}\\tau(z^4),\\\\\n  |\\tau(b(d^2-z^2))|&\\le R^{-2}\\tau(z^4),\\\\\n  \\tau(d^4)&\\le\\tau(z^4).\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{u:eq:clipping-tails}$$ In particular, if $z$ is obtained from Proposition <a href=\"#u:prop:averaged-observable\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"u:prop:averaged-observable\">33</a>, then $\\norm{d}\\le R$ and, for every $\\tau\\in\\Lambda$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n  \\norm{[d,a]}_{2,\\tau}&\\le\\sqrt{72\\eta} &&(a\\in E),\\\\\n  |\\tau(bd)|&\\le3/R^3 &&(b\\in A,\\ \\norm{b}\\le1),\\\\\n  |\\tau(b(d^2-1))|&\\le3/R^2 &&(b\\in A,\\ \\norm{b}\\le1),\\\\\n  \\tau(d^4)&\\le3\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{u:eq:clipped-moments}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-291.4"
        }
